detail_Aging_of_Outstanding_OP_Identified_Remaining_Unrecovered: null
detail_Compliance_Status: Compliant
detail_DNP_Discussion: "Do Not Pay is a governmentwide tool within the U.S. Department\
  \ of the Treasury’s (Treasury) Bureau of the Fiscal Service for federal agencies\
  \ to check data sources to verify a recipient's identity, eligibility, and bank\
  \ account information before a payment is made.\n\nDOI has worked closely with Treasury\
  \ throughout Fiscal Year (FY) 2026 to leverage DNP to prevent improper payments,\
  \ reduce fraud risk, and improve overall payment accuracy.\n\nDOI uses various DNP\
  \ services for its programs.\n\nPayment Verification. Payment Verification is conducted\
  \ on all payment files at the time they are submitted to Treasury. Treasury began\
  \ returning payments matched to deceased individuals on May 27, 2026, and payments\
  \ with missing or invalid Tax Identification Numbers (TINs) and/or invalid bank\
  \ account information on September 30, 2026. According to Treasury’s DNP Portal,\
  \ DOI screened an estimated 10.5 million payments totaling $40 billion during FY\
  \ 2026. Treasury's records show that this includes an estimated 9 million payments\
  \ totaling almost $19 billion made by the Interior Business Center Human Resource\
  \ Directorate, which processes payroll on behalf of DOI and several other federal\
  \ entities. Through the use of Payment Verification 31 DOI payments totaling $111,000\
  \ were identified as improper payments that were prevented.\n\nPre-award and Pre-payment\
  \ Verification. Various DOI programs use the Web Portal Search on an ad hoc basis.\
  \ Based on Treasury's reporting, over 3k records were screened through the Web Portal\
  \ Search in FY 2026.  Continuous monitoring was also used weekly and monthly by\
  \ different DOI programs. Treasury's reporting indicated that over 11M records were\
  \ screened through Continuous Monitoring in FY 2026. Through the use of DNP Continuous\
  \ Monitoring, DOI estimates that it prevented over $8M from being disbursed improperly.\n\
  \nDOI has established an Application Programming Interface (API) connection to DNP\
  \ to utilize when performing pre-award and pre-payment verification. To establish\
  \ the connection DOI has completed the following tasks in FY 2026:\n•\tDeveloped\
  \ the DOI Program Access List with over 500 agency programs to determine which programs\
  \ must use DNP, what access is needed for each program to comply with OMB M-25-32,\
  \ Preventing Improper Payments and Protecting Privacy Through Do Not Pay, and which\
  \ matching programs (if any) are eligible for the matching agreement waiver under\
  \ 5 U.S.C. § 552a(o), Matching Agreements, pursuant to OMB M-25-32\n•\tReviewed\
  \ and updated applicable System of Records Notices on the Federal Register.\n•\t\
  Signed data sharing agreement governing the terms and conditions for DOI’s access\
  \ to DNP.\n•\tMaintained department-wide communication to emphasize the importance\
  \ of using DNP through the payment cycle."

detail_Executive_Summary: 'On March 2, 2020, the Payment Integrity Information Act
  of 2019, (PIIA) was enacted into law (P.L.116-117). This law strengthens efforts
  to combat improper payments by consolidating previous improper payment legislation
  and reinforcing payment reporting requirements within the federal government. On
  March 5, 2021, OMB issued an update to Appendix C of Circular A-123 in Memorandum
  M-21-19, Requirements for Payment Integrity Improvement, which established specific
  requirements for enhancing payment integrity, including the criteria agencies must
  follow to remain compliant with PIIA. This includes conducting Phase 1 Risk Assessments,
  and Phase 2 Statistical Sample Testing of high-risk programs.


  To further strengthen efforts to prevent improper payments and fraud, Executive
  Order (EO) 14247, Modernizing Payments to and From America’s Bank Account and EO
  14249, Protecting America’s Bank Account Against Fraud, Waste, and Abuse, were signed
  by the President on March 25, 2025. EO 14249 aims to strengthen government‑wide
  payment screening, financial system management, and interagency data sharing. EO
  14247 directs federal agencies to transition to electronic payments and collections,
  phasing out most paper checks by September 30, 2025, to enhance security of Federal
  payments, reduce costs, and lower the risk of fraud and improper payments.


  The PIIA requires agencies to review programs and activities for the risk of improper
  payment by performing risk assessments to determine whether those programs are susceptible
  to significant improper payments. In accordance with PIIA, DOI follows an assessment
  schedule, under which each applicable program is reviewed once every three years.
  In addition to this regular cycle, DOI performs off-cycle assessments for programs
  that are newly established or have experienced significant changes, such as new
  legislation or substantial increases in funding levels, that may increase their
  potential for improper payments. With the Office of Management and Budget (OMB)
  concurrence, DOI utilizes an alternative annual assessment period spanning from
  the fourth quarter (Q4) of the prior fiscal year through the third quarter (Q3)
  of the current fiscal year.


  In FY 2025, DOI conducted an in-cycle risk assessment for 103 programs identified
  within the Federal Account Symbols and Titles (FAST Book). Each of these programs
  had payments exceeding $10 million during the assessment period, which covered Q4
  of FY 2024 through Q3 of FY 2025, with a combined total of approximately $33.3 billion.
  The assessments determined that none of the programs were at high risk for improper
  payments, and as a result, all DOI programs remained in Phase 1. Additionally, OIG
  conducted its annual inspection and determined that the Department was in compliance
  with PIIA.


  As part of the government‑wide Do Not Pay (DNP) onboarding initiative led by the
  Department of the Treasury in FY 2026, DOI developed a Program Access List (PAL)
  that identifies more than 500 programs using Federal Program Identifiers (FPIs)
  and FAST Book IDs. This effort significantly reshaped the set of PIIA programs that
  DOI assesses and reports on. In consultation with OMB, DOI conducted risk assessments
  for programs listed in the FAST Book and reported the results on PaymentAccuracy.gov
  in alignment with the PAL.


  In FY 2026, DOI completed an off‑cycle risk assessment of 16 programs identified
  in the FAST Book, representing approximately $4.44 billion in payments during the
  assessment period. None of the assessed programs were identified as high risk for
  significant improper payments, and no programs were selected for Appendix C Phase
  2 Testing during the fiscal year. To support reporting based on the PAL, in compliance
  with OMB reporting requirements, DOI aligned the risk assessment outcomes with the
  PAL program structure, resulting in reporting across 21 programs. Additional information
  regarding DOI’s PIIA and Appendix C payment integrity reporting and compliance activities—including
  Phase 1 Risk Assessments and prior‑year Phase 2 Testing results—is available on
  PaymentAccuracy.gov.'

detail_Recovery_Not_Cost_Effective_Justification: 'The Department conducts recovery
  activities in accordance with OMB Circular A-123 as part of its efforts to strengthen
  payment integrity and reduce improper payments. These activities include internal
  control and Single Audit reviews.


  The DOI will continue to leverage these efforts to address improper payments in
  a cost-effective and efficient manner while also assessing whether implementing
  a payment recapture audit program would provide additional value or benefits.'
